MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Jeanie Shrednik, Parks and Greenway Planner 
Subject: Grant of Utility Easement between Cheyenne Light, Fuel and Power Company DBA Black 
Hills Energy located on a portion of Lot 8, Centennial Heritage, 2nd Filing on City owned 
property 
Date: 
June 17, 2024 
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is a grant of 244+/- SF utility easement to Cheyenne Light, Fuel and Power 
Company DBA Black Hills Energy for utility facilities on a portion of Lot 8, Block 1, Centennial Heritage, 
2nd Filing.  The requested easement is located in the area of a future Greenway and language has been 
added for required bury depth of utility facilities to accommodate future Greenway construction.  Both 
the agreement and exhibit have been reviewed and approved by City Surveyor 
 
The Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral 06/24/2024 
• 
Public Services Committee 07/02/2024 
• 
Final Reading 07/08/2024 
 
Attachments: 
1. Grant of Access Easement 
2. Easement Exhibit 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

UTILITY EASEMENT 
 
 
THIS EASEMENT is made and entered into this _____ day of ____________, 2024, by 
and between the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming (“City”), a Municipal Corporation, its 
successors and assigns, (hereinafter “Grantor”), and Cheyenne Light, Fuel and Power 
Company DBA Black Hills Energy; having an address of 1301 West 24th Street, Cheyenne, 
WY 82001 (hereinafter “Grantee”). 
 
 
Grantor, in consideration of the sum of Ten Dollars ($10.00) and other valuable 
consideration, the receipt of which is hereby acknowledged, hereby grants, bargains, sells and 
conveys to Grantee, its lessees, licensees, successors and assigns, a perpetual right-of-way and 
utility easement for an UNDERGROUND ELECTRIC POWER DISTRIBUTION LINE(S), 
collectively “Facilities”; and all appurtenances and appliances necessary in connection 
therewith, including but not limited to any and all necessary attachments and appurtenances for 
said Facilities;  
 
 
TOGETHER WITH the power to extend to any communications company, or “dry” 
utilities provider, the right to use any trench or conduit placed pursuant to the provisions hereof. 
 
 
AND TOGETHER WITH the right, privilege and perpetual right-of-way to survey, 
construct, operate, patrol, inspect, maintain, alter, repair, rebuild, upgrade, up-rate, enhance, 
replace, and remove said facilities, under and over said lands, now or at any future time, and the 
right of ingress and egress to and from the Facilities of Grantee. Said Easement being more 
particularly described as follows: 
 
An 10-foot-wide utility easement being more particularly described as: 
 
All of the West 10 feet of Lot 8, Block 1, Centennial Heritage, 2nd Filing, City of 
Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming.  
 
Said Easement containing approximately 244 square feet more or less. 
 
As a further consideration of this agreement, the parties hereto agree as follows: 
 
1. 
Grantee has selected the route for said Facilities over and through said premises, 
and shall be responsible for all construction, reconstruction, operation, installation, use, repair, 
replacement, upkeep, monitoring, and maintenance of the Facilities, 
 
2. 
That Grantee will bury all Facilities a minimum of 36 inches below existing 
ground to allow for construction of future Greenway trail, 
 
3. 
That Grantee will replace or rebuild any and all unusual or excessive damage to 
the easement property, and/or pay for any damage caused which shall be occasioned by the 
construction, operation and maintenance of said Facilities under and through the above-described 
premises, 
 
11:38 am, Jun 05 2024

4. 
Grantee, following the installation, maintenance or removal of the Facilities, shall 
restore the surface of the Permanent Easement and any constructed improvements to, as near as 
practicable, the condition of the surface and the constructed improvements prior to said 
installation or maintenance, 
 
5. 
If any stakeholder requires relocation of the Facilities (including, but not limited 
to Grantor, Grantee AND the City of Cheyenne, Utilities Franchisor and Right-of-Way and 
Greenway Administrator) Grantee agrees to relocate the Facilities at its own expense subject to 
the following: 
 
a. 
Grantor provides a suitable alternate location for such Facilities and grants 
the necessary easement rights at the new location upon the same terms and 
conditions herein provided. 
 
It is further understood and agreed that this agreement shall run with the land and shall 
inure to the benefit of, and be binding upon the legal representatives, heirs, devisees, 
successors and assigns of the respective parties, and by the execution and 
acknowledgment thereof, Grantors jointly and severally waive their homestead rights to 
the above-described lands so far as the same may be affected by this agreement. 
 
It is the intention of the parties hereto that Grantor is hereby conveying the uses herein 
specified without divesting himself, his heirs or assigns, of the right to use and enjoy the 
above described premises:  PROVIDED, however, such use shall not, in the judgment of 
said Grantee, interfere with or endanger the construction, safety, operation or 
maintenance of said lines, and provided further that no building shall be constructed on 
the easement without written permission from Grantee. 
 
Title to said lines shall be and remain in said Grantee. 
 
Grantor hereby represents, warrants and covenants that Grantor is the sole owner(s) of the 
above-described land, subject to existing liens and easements of record and has all rights to grant 
this easement.

CONTRACT NO. _________ 
 
MEMORANDUM OF UNDERSTANDING 
BETWEEN THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
AND CHEYENNE FRONTIER DAYS, INC. 
 
This Memorandum of Understanding ("MOU") is made and executed this _______ day of 
_________________, 2024, by and between the City of Cheyenne, a municipality duly organized 
and existing under the laws of the State of Wyoming ("City"), whose address is 2101 O’Neil 
Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001, and Cheyenne Frontier Days, Inc., a Wyoming corporation 
("CFD"), whose address is P.O. Box 2477, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82003, in which the parties agree 
as follows: 
 
RECITALS 
 
1. 
The City owns land and buildings near Frontier Park, which have been used for parking, 
camping, and other activities by CFD.  Specifically, the City owns: 
 
a. 
Land around Lake Absarraca, which is described as that area bordered by Kennedy 
Road on the south, Interstate 25 on the west, the Governor's Mansion property on 
the northwest corner, and the Airport Golf Course on the north, northeast and east; 
 
b. 
The “E lot” parking area described as north of Lions Park Drive, south of the 
Municipal Aquatics Center, and east of Carey Avenue; and 
 
c. 
Paved parking lots located adjacent to the Boy Scouts' meeting house, between 
Sloan's Lake and the Western Walkway, which for the purposes of this agreement 
are named CFD lots. 
 
2. 
The City and CFD have, in the past, entered into agreements regarding the use of the City 
owned parcels and structures.  This MOU is substantially similar to earlier agreements. 
 
3. 
During the 2024 Cheyenne Frontier Days celebration, CFD proposes to: 
 
a. 
Provide overnight camping at Lake Absarraca, from the Sunday preceding the 
Cheyenne Frontier Days celebration through the Monday after the celebration ends; 
 
b. 
Provide public parking at the CFD lot.  There will be no overnight parking in the 
CDF lot.  This additional parking for automobiles in the confines of Lions Park is 
to facilitate access to the Cheyenne Frontier Days Old West Museum and relieve 
parking pressure in the Avenues; 
 
c. 
Provide for storage of towed vehicles at the CFD lot; and 
 
 
WHEREFORE, in consideration of the mutual promises by the City and CFD made each

to the other, the parties agree as follows:

b.

c. 
Mark and designate camping and parking slots to maximize use of the space; 
 
d. 
Provide car parking and revenue collectors; 
 
e. 
Allow for public access to Lions Park along Lions Park Drive during the 
celebration; 
 
f.

g.

It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory.  It 
shall be an affirmative obligation upon CFD to immediately notify in writing the 
city risk manager, city clerk, and city attorney of any fact, circumstances, or 
occurrence that has resulted in or may result in the cancellation or substantive 
change of any insurance coverage required by this MOU, and failure to do so shall 
be construed to be a breach of this MOU. 
 
The City of Cheyenne shall be named as an additional insured on CFD's insurance 
policies, except Workers' Compensation, and CFD shall provide, upon request, a 
copy of an endorsement providing this coverage. 
 
In addition, CFD shall provide the City with copies of insurance policies and/or 
policy endorsements listing the City of Cheyenne as an additional insured.  The 
City's failure to request or review such policies, endorsements, or certificates shall 
not affect the City’s right or CFD's obligation hereunder. 
 
The City has the right to reject a certificate of insurance if CFD's insurance 
company is widely regarded in the insurance industry as financially unstable.  Any 
insurance company providing coverage under this MOU shall have a minimum 
A.M. Best rating of A- (Excellent); 
 
k.

l. 
Not allow permit parking; all parking will be fully paid; 
 
m.

Frontier Days celebration; 
 
n. 
Provide an accounting of revenue and expenses for all revenue producing areas 
following the end of the 2024 Cheyenne Frontier Days celebration.  Such 
accounting shall be submitted to the City Clerk no later than November 1, 2024; 
and 
 
 
 
2. 
The City will: 
 
a. 
Provide adequate dumpsters for the areas covered by this MOU and empty daily; 
 
b. 
Provide daily maintenance of associated restroom facilities including all supplies, 
Excluding the facilities at Lake Absarraca which will be maintained by CFD; 
 
c. 
Assist with T-post installation for fencing of the areas, as decided by the parties 
prior to the Monday preceding Cheyenne Frontier Days; and 
 

 
Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and Cheyenne Frontier Days, Inc. 
Page 4 of 5 
 
3. 
The parties agree that the first Fourteen Thousand Five Hundred Dollars ($14,500.00) to 
be generated from the parking lot and camping rentals will be paid over to CFD as the 
City's contribution to the annual 4th of July fireworks celebration. The remaining half of 
the fees, in an amount not to exceed Fourteen Thousand Five Hundred Dollars ($14,500.00) 
will be paid by CFD. 
 
4. 
It is further agreed that the remainder of the parking lot and camping net revenue, after the 
contribution to the 4th of July fireworks celebration and payment of expenses associated 
with collection of parking and camping revenue, shall be divided equally between the 
parties and CFD shall deliver to the City, the City's revenue portion with the accounting of 
revenue and expenses pursuant to Section 1(m) of this MOU.  The remainder of the City's 
portion of these funds will be deposited into the City's Parks Project Fund. 
 
5. 
The City agrees to rent the Tow Lot, CFD Lot, and Lake Absarraca Campground to CFD 
for One Dollar ($1.00). 
 
6. 
This MOU shall commence June 1, 2024, and terminate August 31, 2024, unless sooner 
terminated by either party upon thirty (30) days written notice.  This shall not affect CFD's 
obligations under Section 1(m) of this MOU. 
 
7. 
CFD agrees to comply with all applicable federal, state and local laws, rules and 
regulations. 
 
8. 
The City expressly invokes its governmental immunity for any claim arising out of this 
MOU pursuant to the Wyoming Governmental Claims Act, Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et seq. 
 
9. 
CFD agrees to indemnify, hold harmless and defend the City from and against any and all 
liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures, and suits, and the cost and expenses incident 
thereto including reasonable attorney's fees, which may hereafter arise as a result of death 
or bodily injury to any person, destruction, or damage to any property, contamination of or 
adverse effects on the environment, or any violation of governmental laws, regulations, or 
orders to the extent caused by (1) CFD's breach of any term or provision of this MOU, or 
(2) any negligent or wrongful act, error, or omission by CFD, or its employees or 
subcontractors in the performance of this MOU.  CFD acknowledges that it may incur a 
financial obligation to the City pursuant to the terms of this paragraph. 
 
10. 
This MOU contains the entire understanding of the parties.  There are no other terms or 
conditions, written or oral, concerning or controlling this matter. 
 
11. 
The parties agree that this MOU shall not become effective and enforceable until it has 
been approved by the parties' respective governing bodies. 
 
 
 
 

 
Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and Cheyenne Frontier Days, Inc. 
Page 5 of 5

their names by and through their respective authorized representatives.

CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING 
AGREEMENT 
 
Bid No. S-18-24 
 
Contract No. _______ 
 
THIS AGREEMENT, entered into this ____ day of ___________, 2024, by and between 
the CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, hereinafter referred to as the “CITY”, and BETTER 
LINE ROOFING, hereinafter referred to as the “CONTRACTOR”. 
 
WITNESSETH that the Contractor and the City, for the considerations stated herein, 
mutually agree as follows: 
 
ARTICLE 1.  STATEMENT OF WORK.  The Contractor shall furnish all supervision, 
technical personnel, labor, materials, machinery, tools, equipment, and services, including utility 
and transportation services, and perform and complete all work in an efficient and workmanlike 
manner in the construction of the "Cheyenne Fire Station 1 Roof Replacement" project all in 
strict accordance with the Contract Documents including all addenda thereto, numbered and dated: 
Addendum One (1), dated May 10, 2024, and Addendum Two (2), dated May 16, 2024. 
 
ARTICLE 2.  RESPONSIBLE DESIGNEE FOR THE CITY.  The Contractor shall in any 
and all matters relating to the scope of services to be provided under this Contract or any other 
provisions herein, contact the City Engineer, or his/her designated representative. 
 
ARTICLE 3.  TIME FRAME FOR COMPLETION.  The services to be performed under 
this Agreement shall commence on the date stipulated in the “Notice to Proceed” that will be issued 
by the City.  The work shall be completed by December 31, 2024.  If the work has not been 
completed within the time stipulated above, including any extensions of time issued by the City 
for excusable delays, the Contractor and his/her sureties shall pay the City fixed, agreed liquidated 
damages, as stipulated in the Supplemental Conditions, for each calendar day of delay until the 
work is completed. 
 
ARTICLE 4.  COMPENSATION AND METHOD OF PAYMENT.  The CITY will pay 
the CONTRACTOR for the performance of the Contract in current funds, the sum of Three 
Hundred, Thirty-Nine Thousand Dollars ($339,000.00). In the event there are changes in the 
estimated quantities shown on the Bid Proposal, the unit prices multiplied by the actual quantities 
shall govern, and the total contract amount will be adjusted accordingly.  The City agrees to pay 
the above amount for contractual services in the following manner, upon receipt of appropriate 
documentation: 
 
a. 
The Contractor will be paid on a monthly basis for the percentage of 
estimated work completed.  Submittal will be at least seven (7) business days prior to the 
payable due date as established annually by the City Treasurer’s Office.  The pay request 
shall be submitted on the Contract Payment Request Form and Itemized Pay Request or 
1:49 pm, May 30 2024

Page 2 of 2 
the AIA Documents G702 and G703.  The engineer will review the estimate for approval 
prior to payment. 
b. 
The City will withhold five percent (5%) of the dollar value of the work 
completed for a minimum of forty-one (41) calendar days after Notice of Final Settlement 
has been published in accordance with Wyo. Stat. §16-6-116.  Upon completion of the 
work under this Contract, the Contractor shall submit a Contractor’s Certificate of 
Completion; the Consent of Surety; Final Waivers of Lien from the Contractor, and all 
Sub-Contractors, Suppliers and Materialmen; Affidavit of Release of Liens; Affidavit of 
Payment; and a current Workers Compensation Certificate.  Final payment will not be 
made until the above documents have been received by the City and all items on the Punch 
List have been completed, and the advertising requirements have been met. 
 
ARTICLE 5.  CONTRACT.  The executed Contract Documents shall consist of the 
following: 
 
1. This Agreement;  
2. Addenda; 
3. Invitation for Bids; 
4. Instructions to Bidders; 
5. Signed Bid Proposal; 
 
6. General Conditions and Insurance; 
7. Supplemental Conditions; 
8. Part IV Forms & Notices; 
9. Specifications and Special 
Provisions; and  
10. Drawings. 
 
This Agreement, together with other documents enumerated in this Article 5, which said 
other documents are as fully a part of the Contract as if hereto attached or herein repeated, forms 
the Contract between the parties hereto. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, THAT the governing body of the City of Cheyenne has 
authorized the Mayor as Executive Officer of the City to enter into this Agreement, and that the 
parties hereto have caused this Agreement to be executed on the day and year in the first part herein 
written.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-06-04_TotalExtraction_63854361.pdf
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
June 4, 2024 
SUBJECT: 
Consideration of Bid #S-26-24 for an Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and 
STC Construction Co., Inc for the VA Cemetery Road project 
______________________________________________ 
Bid #S-26-24 was solicited for the VA Cemetery Road project (Project Vicinity Map included). 
 
Four (4) bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.  The Bids were received 
from: 
1. STC Construction Co., Inc 
2. JTL Group Inc. dba Knife River 
3. Martin Marietta 
4. Reiman Corp. 
 
The bids were reviewed by Tom Cobb (Engineering), Cassie Pickett (Engineering), and Erin Gates 
(Engineering) and their recommendation is to accept the lowest responsive bid from STC Construction 
Co., Inc. 
 
The total Bid Award for the Base Bid is $299,237.79.  The City included a 10% Incentive if the 
Contractor reaches Substantial Completion prior to August 1, 2024.  The 10% Incentive totals 
$29,923.78.  Grand total for Base Bid & 10% Incentive is $329,161.57. 
 
Project Completion is scheduled for August 31, 2024, or August 1, 2024 (If Incentive Date is met).

CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING 
AGREEMENT 
 
Bid No. S-26-24 
 
Contract No. _______ 
 
THIS AGREEMENT, entered into this ____ day of ___________, 2024, by and between 
the CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, hereinafter referred to as the “CITY”, and STC 
CONSTRUCTION CO., INC, hereinafter referred to as the “CONTRACTOR”. 
 
WITNESSETH that the Contractor and the City, for the considerations stated herein, 
mutually agree as follows: 
 
ARTICLE 1.  STATEMENT OF WORK.  The Contractor shall furnish all supervision, 
technical personnel, labor, materials, machinery, tools, equipment, and services, including utility 
and transportation services, and perform and complete all work in an efficient and workmanlike 
manner in the construction of the "VA Cemetery Road" project all in strict accordance with the 
Contract Documents including all addenda thereto, numbered and dated: Addendum One (1), 
dated May 24, 2024. 
 
ARTICLE 2.  RESPONSIBLE DESIGNEE FOR THE CITY.  The Contractor shall in any 
and all matters relating to the scope of services to be provided under this Contract or any other 
provisions herein, contact the City Engineer, or his/her designated representative. 
 
ARTICLE 3.  TIME FRAME FOR COMPLETION.  The services to be performed under 
this Agreement shall commence on the date stipulated in the “Notice to Proceed” that will be issued 
by the City.  The work shall be completed by August 1, 2024.  If the Contractor has reached 
Substantial Completion prior to August 31, 2024, the City will pay a Ten (10%) Percent Incentive.  
If the work has not been completed within the time stipulated above, including any extensions of 
time issued by the City for excusable delays, the Contractor and his/her sureties shall pay the City 
fixed, agreed liquidated damages, as stipulated in the Supplemental Conditions, for each calendar 
day of delay until the work is completed. 
 
ARTICLE 4.  COMPENSATION AND METHOD OF PAYMENT.  The CITY will pay 
the CONTRACTOR for the performance of the Contract in current funds, Base Bid amount of 
Two Hundred Ninety-Nine Thousand, Two Hundred Thirty-Seven Dollars, and Seventy-
Nine Cents ($299,237.79), if the Ten (10%) Percent Incentive date is met, the City will pay 
an amount not to exceed Twenty-Nine Thousand, Nine Hundred Twenty-Three Dollars, and 
Seventy-Eight Cents ($29,923.78), for a grand total of Three Hundred Twenty-Nine 
Thousand, One Hundred Sixty-One Dollars, and Fifty-Seven Cents ($329,161.57). In the event 
there are changes in the estimated quantities shown on the Bid Proposal, the unit prices multiplied 
by the actual quantities shall govern, and the total contract amount will be adjusted accordingly.  
The City agrees to pay the above amount for contractual services in the following manner, upon 
receipt of appropriate documentation: 
 
10:04 am, Jun 05 2024

Page 2 of 3 
a. 
The Contractor will be paid on a monthly basis for the percentage of 
estimated work completed.  Submittal will be at least seven (7) business days prior to the 
payable due date as established annually by the City Treasurer’s Office.  The pay request 
shall be submitted on the Contract Payment Request Form and Itemized Pay Request or 
the AIA Documents G702 and G703.  The engineer will review the estimate for approval 
prior to payment. 
 
b. 
The City will withhold five percent (5%) of the dollar value of the work 
completed for a minimum of forty-one (41) calendar days after Notice of Final Settlement 
has been published in accordance with Wyo. Stat. §16-6-116.  Upon completion of the 
work under this Contract, the Contractor shall submit a Contractor’s Certificate of 
Completion; the Consent of Surety; Final Waivers of Lien from the Contractor, and all 
Sub-Contractors, Suppliers and Materialmen; Affidavit of Release of Liens; Affidavit of 
Payment; and a current Workers Compensation Certificate.  Final payment will not be 
made until the above documents have been received by the City and all items on the Punch 
List have been completed, and the advertising requirements have been met. 
 
ARTICLE 5.  CONTRACT.  The executed Contract Documents shall consist of the 
following: 
 
1. This Agreement;  
2. Addenda; 
3. Invitation for Bids; 
4. Instructions to Bidders; 
5. Signed Bid Proposal; 
 
6. General Conditions and Insurance; 
7. Supplemental Conditions; 
8. Part IV Forms & Notices; 
9. Specifications and Special 
Provisions; and  
10. Drawings. 
 
This Agreement, together with other documents enumerated in this Article 5, which said 
other documents are as fully a part of the Contract as if hereto attached or herein repeated, forms 
the Contract between the parties hereto. 
 
 

Page 3 of 3 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, THAT the governing body of the City of Cheyenne has 
authorized the Mayor as Executive Officer of the City to enter into this Agreement, and that the 
parties hereto have caused this Agreement to be executed on the day and year in the first part herein 
written. 
 
ATTEST:

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
June 18, 2024 
SUBJECT: 
Consideration of Bid #S-25-24 for an Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and 
Wescom, Inc for the Norris Viaduct Lighting Modifications & I-180 Pedestrian Bridge 
Lighting project 
______________________________________________ 
Bid #S-25-24 was solicited for the Norris Viaduct Lighting Modifications & I-180 Pedestrian Bridge 
Lighting project (Project Vicinity Map included). 
 
One (1) bidder responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.  The Bid was received from: 
1. Wescom, Inc. 
 
The bid was reviewed by Tom Cobb (Engineering), and Rick Amen (Engineering) and their 
recommendation is to accept the lowest responsive bid from Wescom, Inc. 
 
The total Bid Award for the Base Bid is $164,772.00.  Project Completion by June 27, 2025.  The City 
included a 10% Incentive if the Contractor reaches Substantial Completion prior to December 13, 
2024.  The 10% Incentive totals $16,477.20.  Grand total for Base Bid & 10% Incentive is 
$181,249.20. 
 
Funding:  
020-26-2617-42-43506 (1% Sales Tax / Traffic and Safety Improvements) 
 
 

 
 
 

 
City of Cheyenne 
Purchasing

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-05-14_TotalExtraction_82819667.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
April 22, 2024  
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich (via Zoom), Bryan Cook, Ken 
Esquibel, Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn (arriving at 6:01 p.m.), Dr. Mark Rinne, Tom Segrave 
and Jeff White.  Absent: Scott Roybal.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of 
allegiance was recited. 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda).  Mr. Esquibel moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion 
carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on April 8, 2024.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for the City’s Annual Action Plan for Program Year 
2024 prepared by the Cheyenne Housing and Community Development Division.  There being no 
further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the 
hearing.  Public comment was made by: Dan Dorsch, Executive Director, Habitat for Humanity of 
Laramie County, Angie Dorsch, Executive Director, Equal Justice Wyoming and Kristin-Erin 
Balderaz, Executive Director, Needs, Inc.  Comments were made by the following members of the 
governing body: Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Esquibel. 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from MUB Mixed-Use Business Emphasis to CB 
Community Business for land located at Lot 2, Block 1, Q-B Subdivision. Reporting for Public 
Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.       
 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, High 
Plains Road beginning at South Greeley Highway and terminating at Division Avenue.  Dr. Rinne 
moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body present. 
       
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located east of Saratoga Street and west of Whitney Road.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present. 

 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne establishing the zoning classification of AR Agricultural Residential for land annexed 
to the City of Cheyenne located east of Saratoga Street and south of and adjacent to Laramie Street.  
Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present.   
 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located at 4204 Dell Range Boulevard.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded 
by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.       
    
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne establishing the zoning classification of City MR Medium Density Residential for land 
annexed to the City of Cheyenne located at 4204 Dell Range Boulevard. Dr. Rinne moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present.       
 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from CB Community Business to P Public District 
for land located at Lot 1, Cheyenne Ice & Events Center, 2nd Filing located at 1530 West 
Lincolnway.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. Comment was made by the 
following member of the governing body: Dr. Rinne. 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Sections 1.4.3 Defined Terms and 5.8.8 
Accessory Buildings of the Unified Development Code, clarifying Detached Buildings – referred 
to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal)         
 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Ordinance No. 4519 amending the Official 
Zoning Map of the City of Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from HI Heavy Industrial, 
AG Agricultural, and County A-2 to LI Light Industrial for land located west of Burlington Trail 
and south of and adjacent to HR Ranch Road – referred to Public Services Committee.  (Sponsor 
– Mr. Roybal)         
 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Subsections 6.5.4.b.3 Incentives / Bonus, 
6.5.4.b.4 Creative Sign Program, and 6.5.8.j Billboards of the Unified Development Code, to 
restructure sign incentives and bonuses by modifying allowances for signs near interstate 
highways, allowances for shared signs, eliminating the Creative Sign Program, and clarifying 
applicability of certain billboard allowances – referred to Public Services Committee.  (Sponsor – 
Mr. Roybal)   
 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Section 5.1.5 of the Unified Development 
Code to allow for up to three stories for detached dwellings, semi-attached dwellings, and attached 
dwellings – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal)         
 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending and updating the Solid Waste Fee Schedule 
in accordance with Section 8.44.010 of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, 
and repealing Ordinance No. 4503 – referred to Finance Committee.  (Sponsor – Dr. Aldrich)          

 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending Sections 10.82.030 and 10.82.040 of the 
Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, modifying the term of Dockless Vehicle 
licenses, specifying an effective date and making other conforming amendments – referred to 
Finance Committee. (Sponsors – Mr. Roybal, Mr. Segrave and Mr. White) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Expressing the support of the Governing Body for the adoption of the 
Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
(#6398) 
 
 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Carey 
Cottages, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located southeast of the intersection of 
Grove Drive and Cheyenne Street).  Dr. Rinne moved to adopt with staff recommendations 1 (a) 
and (b) and 2 (a) and (b) which includes acknowledgements of relief of access to a street for lots 
less than 36’ wide (Subdivision Standards Waiver) and a reduction in a rear setback 
(Administrative Adjustment); and prior to recording a 10-foot utility easement be added along 
Cheyenne Street and a public access easement be added along Cheyenne Street for a 6-foot 
attached sidewalk, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present.  Public comment was made by: Brandon Swain, applicant and Dan 
Dorsch, Vice Chair, Affordable Housing Task Force. (#6399)

Rise Hills North Subdivision, 3rd Filing, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming.  Dr. Rinne 
moved to adopt with staff recommendation to grant the exception to maximum block size, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”. Public comment was made by: Elizabeth 
Stapel, Rebecca Murchie, Mark Christensen, AVI, representative for the applicant and Dan 
Dorsch, Vice Chair, Affordable Housing Task Force.  Comments were made by the following 
members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Cook, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Segrave and Mr. White.

Subject: PUDC-24-51 | 3421 Gregg Way – Zoning Map Amendment

Background:

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION 
FROM MUB MIXED-USE BUSINESS EMPHASIS TO CB COMMUNITY 
BUSINESS FOR LAND LOCATED AT LOT 2, BLOCK 1, Q-B 
SUBDIVISION." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a 
zoning map amendment for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map 
Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code ("UDC"). 
 
 
Section 2.  That on April 1, 2024, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted 
a public hearing, reviewed the proposed zoning map amendment that is the subject of this 
ordinance, and recommended approval of said zoning map amendment. 
 
 
Section 3.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zoning map 
amendment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of 
the UDC. 
 
 
Section 4.  That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, 
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the 
aforementioned application is hereby approved, and the zoning classification of the real property 
described as Lot 2, Block 1, Q-B Subdivision, Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby changed to CB 
Community Business. 
 
 
Section 5.  That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zoning map 
amendment as described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with 
Section 7 below. 
 
 
Section 6.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 7.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication.

Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   (Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION 
APRIL 1, 2024 
STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
UDC-24-51: 3324 Gregg Way Zone Change  
 
REQUEST: 
A request to rezone approximately 1.23 acres of land from MUB Mixed-
use Business to CB Community Business. 
LOCATION: 
North of Dell Range Boulevard between Ridge Road and College Avenue  
AGENT: 
OWNER: 
Casey Palma 
Cheyenne Real Estate LLC Et Al 
ZONING: 
MUB Mixed-use Business  
FUTURE LAND USE: 
Mixed-use Commercial 
PURPOSE: 
Rezoning to CB to allow automobile service station as a use-by right and with 
fewer design standards. 
PREPARED BY: 
Seth Lloyd, Senior Planner  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PUDC-24-51 
 
 
April 1, 2024 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of Lot 2, Block 1, Q-B Subdivision to CB Community Business, noting that 
the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
• 
UDC Article 5 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from MUB Mixed-use Business to CB Community 
Business. The land is currently an outdoor storage area for a utility company. The purpose of the proposed 
zone change is to allow for automobile service use as a use-by-right with more appropriate design standards.  
 
Shortly after the adoption of the UDC, Staff identified various areas of the community as “mis-zoned”. The 
UDC kept the same zone names and zone locations as Title 17, but the intent and applicability had changed 
significantly, and some areas of the community carried forward zones for their properties which were not 
optimal based on the new UDC language. One of the two areas specifically identified for change in the 2014 
UDC annual review was the King Soopers shopping center. It was stated then that the entire area should be 
zoned CB, not MUB. Unfortunately, Staff never had the time or resources to do a comprehensive rezoning of 
the entire community, electing to go with potential ‘spot zoning’ in the interim to address properties where 
issues arise. 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Future Land Use Designation

PUDC-24-51 
 
 
April 1, 2024 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
The Future Land Use designation for this property is Mixed-use Commercial. The Mixed‐Use 
Commercial category is intended to promote a range of land uses, with primarily retail, office, 
light industrial, and live/work designed as an “activity center”. Parks, plazas and/or open space 
should also be part of the core of Mixed‐Use Commercial areas. 
 
Policies in the comprehensive plan encourage commercial development near activity centers, 
and the intersection of Dell Range and Ridge is considered an activity center by the plan.   
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
There is CB zoning to the southeast of this property. The surrounding area consists of a mix of 
different residential and service uses. The CB zone would be compatible with the nearby 
properties as it is a commercial zone with slightly different standards than the MUB zone. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The surrounding area better matches the expected development patterns of CB zoning. The 
MUB zone requires buildings to be set closer to the streets and have a certain amount of 
windows and other building design elements, whereas the CB zone pushes buildings further 
from the street and requires fewer building design elements. The CB zone is also meant for 
more vehicle-oriented areas which would be the case for Dell Range, Ridge, and College.   
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
This is in an area with an existing street network. The CB zone generally anticipates larger blocks 
and is better suited to the likely development patterns in this situation.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 

PUDC-24-51 
 
 
April 1, 2024 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The CB District is intended for Retail, Employment, Service and Civic uses that may serve a 
number of residential neighborhoods or other districts in meeting daily needs, discretionary 
needs, or other business functions. The district regulations are designed to encourage these 
uses, while also protecting the abutting and nearby areas.  
 
The CB District is applicable in areas where moderate- or large-scale business uses are 
appropriate, specifically larger Mixed-use Commercial Activity Centers, Community/Regional 
Activity Centers, or Community Business areas of the Comprehensive Plan. 
 
This property is close to the Ridge Road and Dell Range intersection and the existing 
development is more low-density, spread-out – better meeting the Intent and design objectives 
of the CB zone, than those of the MUB zone.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The specific proposal for this project is a automobile repair facility. However, if that project is 
not executed after approval, the CB zone offers a wide variety of potential auto-oriented uses at 
this site (including apartments).   
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No major concerns were raised by other revieing bodies. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with this review criterion. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 

PUDC-24-51 
 
 
April 1, 2024 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process no comments were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The proposed 
CB zone is consistent with the comprehensive plan and is appropriate based on the existing conditions 
and potential development patterns for the area.  
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from MUB to CB. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Application 
4. Zone Change Map (from applicant)

has petitioned the governing body of the City of Cheyenne to annex the real 
property described herein and to make it a part thereof; 
 
(b) 
That an accurate annexation map of said land is attached to this ordinance as 
Exhibit A, said map prepared by Scott Bruce, a Professional Land Surveyor, 
licensed in the State of Wyoming, PLS Number 16571; 
 
(c) 
That the annexation of said area is for the protection of health, safety, and welfare 
of the persons residing in the area and the City of Cheyenne; 
 
(d) 
That the urban development of the area sought to be annexed will constitute a 
natural, geographical, economical and social part of the City of Cheyenne; 
 
(e) 
That the area is a logical and feasible addition to the City and that the extension of 
basic services such as water and sewer systems, police and fire protection and 
other services customarily available in the City of Cheyenne can, within reason, 
be furnished; 
 
(f) 
That the City of Cheyenne does not own or operate its own electrical utility, and 
its governing body is prepared to designate a utility to serve the annexed area 
pursuant to W.S. 15-1-410, and to authorize the designated utility to serve the 

entire annexed area. 
 
Section 2.  That all of the real property described in Exhibit A is hereby annexed to the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and shall become a part thereof.  
 
Section 3.  That the City Clerk is directed to file an executed copy of this annexation 
ordinance and map (Exhibit A) with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for 
Laramie County, Wyoming, and shall also notify the City Engineer's Office to add said area to 
the official City Map of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
Section 4.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared 
illegal or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the 
remaining phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.  
 
Section 5.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
PUDC-24-25: High Plains Road - Annexation 
REQUEST: 
An annexation of 7.23 acres of High Plains Road and a small portion of Division 
Avenue. 
LOCATION: 
West of South Greeley Highway and north of Richard Road  
APPLICANT: 
Justin Arnold, Laramie County 
OWNERS: 
Laramie County, Wyoming 
PURPOSE: 
City control and maintenance of the street 
PREPARED BY: 
Sophia Maes, Planner I 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PUDC-24-25 
 
 
April 8, 2024 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
• 
UDC 2.4.2 Annexations 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
BACKGROUND: 
The applicant requests the annexation of 7.23 acres of land. The land is currently a partially developed 
county-owned road (fully developed on the east portion, undeveloped on the west portion).  It connects to 
South Greeley Highway on the east and will be connected to Division Avenue on the west once fully 
constructed. 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
To accept the petition to annex and approve the annexation Ordinance, the Governing Body must make 
the following findings required in WS 15-1-402; a detailed analysis of which is below: 
1) An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety and welfare of the 
persons residing in the area and in the city or town.  [WS 15-1-402 (a) (i)] 
The annexation of this property will increase the health, safety, and welfare of the future 
occupants. By being within City limits, the property will receive access to City Fire and Police and 
road maintenance services.  
The application complies with this review criterion.

PUDC-24-25 
 
 
April 8, 2024 
2) The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would constitute a natural, 
geographical, economic, and social part of the annexing city or town. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (ii)] 
This property will constitute a natural, geographic, economic, and social part of the city. The 
property is a direct connection for the High Plains Business Park (948.88 acres of City property 
and the site of Project Cosmo) to South Greeley Highway. Annexation of this property will 
benefit those who live within the City and will be a part of the social and economic fabric that is 
Cheyenne.   
The application complies with this review criterion. 
3) The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing city or town 
and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents of the city or 
town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed. [WS 15-1-402 (a) 
(iii)] 
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City 
services normally available to residents will also be made available. Police and fire protection 
will be extended upon annexation. Utility infrastructure exists adjacent to the property in 
Division Avenue. 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
4) The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city or town, or 
the area meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (iv)] 
The area proposed for annexation is about 8.3% contiguous. It is directly adjacent to High Plains 
Business Park (948.88 acres of City parcel) and is contiguous via the intersection of High Plains 
Road and Division Avenue. 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
5) If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body is 
prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (v)] 
Utility franchises are in place to serve the annexed area.   
The application complies with this review criterion. 
6) The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all landowners and affected public 
utilities within the territory a summary of the proposed annexation report as required under 
subsection (c) of this section and notice of the time, date and location of the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a). [WS 15-1-402 (a) (vi)] 
A certified mailing was made to the landowners and public utilities on March 8th, 2024. The 
public hearing for this annexation is scheduled for April 8th, 2024. Twenty (20) business days (on 
the City’s calendar) prior to the announced public hearing was March 11th, 2024. The earliest 
confirmation of delivery was dated March 11th, 2024. 
The application complies with this review criterion.   

PUDC-24-25 
 
 
April 8, 2024 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
Plan Cheyenne 
The Future Land Use Map of PlanCheyenne, as updated by Connect 2045, anticipates this area of the 
community to develop as urban residential. These areas can develop with or without access to urban 
services. The area is within the Urban Service Boundary. 
STAFF REVIEW: 
Through the review process, no comments were received. 
CONCLUSION: 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the annexation.   
The proposed annexation is a natural extension of the City, is adjacent and contiguous (8.3%) to the 
current City limits, and services typically available to urban areas are available to be extended to this 
property.  
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
Staff recommends approval of the annexation of the property. 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Future Land Use Map 
2. Submitted Application 
3. Annexation Petition  
4. Applicant-submitted annexation map

Zoning Assignment Review Criteria
The review criterion for a zoning assignment:
"1. The proposed zoning assignment is the most analogous City zone to the current
zone when taking into consideration allowed uses. Zoning for standard county zones
shall be assigned based on the policy document, while zones not listed on the policy
document and terminated City zones shall be assigned a City zone on a case-by-
case basis." (UDC 2.2.8.d.1)

basic services such as water and sewer systems, police and fire protection and 
other services customarily available in the City of Cheyenne can, within reason, 
be furnished; 
 
(f) 
That the area is adjacent to the City of Cheyenne and is currently zoned AR 
Agricultural Residential by the Laramie County Commissioners; and 
 
(g) 
That the City of Cheyenne does not own or operate its own electrical utility, and 
its governing body is prepared to designate a utility to serve the annexed area 
pursuant to W.S. 15-1-410, and to authorize the designated utility to serve the 
entire annexed area. 
 
Section 2.  That all of the real property described in Exhibit A is hereby annexed to the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and shall become a part thereof.  
 
Section 3.  That the City Clerk is directed to file an executed copy of this annexation 
ordinance and map (Exhibit A) with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for 
Laramie County, Wyoming, and shall also notify the City Engineer's Office to add said area to 
the official City Map of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
Section 4.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared 
illegal or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the 
remaining phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.  
 
Section 5.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

STAFF REPORT
FILE:
PUDC-24-29: Laramie Street - Annexation
REQUEST:
An annexation of 9.30 acres of land
LOCATION:
East of Saratoga St and West of Whitney Road. 
APPLICANT: 
Cotton Jones
AGENT:
Cotton Jones
OWNERS: 
Hilltop Funding Inc.
PURPOSE: 
Future development within City Limits. 
PREPARED BY:
Connor White, Planner II
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

PUDC-24-29 
 
 
April 8, 2024 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
x 
UDC 2.4.2 Annexations 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting annexing 9.30 acres of land to allow future develop within City Limits. The land is 
currently vacant on the south end and had a trailer park along Laramie Street. The annexed area will be 
assigned a City zone based on the underlying County zone. No other applications have been submitted for 
this property at this time.  
 
Land annexed to the City requires a City zone. The petition to annex has been accompanied by an assigned 
City zone of AR Agricultural Residential.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
To accept the petition to annex and approve the annexation Ordinance, the Governing Body must make 
the following findings required in WS 15-1-402; a detailed analysis of which is below: 
 
1) An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety and welfare of the 
persons residing in the area and in the city or town.  [WS 15-1-402 (a) (i)] 
 
The annexation of this property will increase the health, safety, and welfare of the future 
occupants. By being in City limits, the property will receive access to City Fire and Police and 
sanitation services. The property, if occupied, will also be required to be connected to City water 
and sanitary sewer instead of having wells and septic systems.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
2) The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would constitute a natural, 
geographical, economical and social part of the annexing city or town. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (ii)]

PUDC-24-29 
 
 
April 8, 2024 
This property, once developed, will constitute a natural, geographic, economic, and social part 
of the city. The property is adjacent to the City to the west. The contiguity to the City and the 
proximity to surrounding urban-style development makes the parcel a natural geographic part 
of the City. The development of this property will benefit those who live within the City and will 
be a part of the social and economic fabric that is Cheyenne.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
3) The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing city or town 
and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents of the city or 
town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed. [WS 15-1-402 (a) 
(iii)] 
 
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City 
services normally available to residents will also be made available. Police and fire protection 
will be extended upon annexation. Utilities exist in the area and will be expanded into the 
proposed area as it develops. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city or town, or 
the area meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (iv)] 
 
The area proposed for annexation is about 12% contiguous, along the western and eastern 
edge.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
5) If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body is 
prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (v)] 
 
Utility franchises are in place to serve the annexed area.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
    
6) The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all landowners and affected public 
utilities within the territory a summary of the proposed annexation report as required under 
subsection (c) of this section and notice of the time, date and location of the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a). [WS 15-1-402 (a) (vi)] 
 
A certified mailing was made to the landowners and public utilities on March 8, 2024. The public 
hearing for this annexation is scheduled for April 8, 2024. Twenty (20) business days (on the 
City’s calendar) prior to the announced public hearing is March 11, 2024. The earliest 
confirmation of delivery was dated March 11, 2024. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
 

PUDC-24-29 
 
 
April 8, 2024 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
Plan Cheyenne 
 
The Future Land Use Map of PlanCheyenne, as updated by Connect 2045, anticipates this area of the 
community to develop with urban residential uses. These areas are expected to develop with full access 
to urban services – such areas are best served by being within City limits. The area is within the Urban 
Service Boundary. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, no comments were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the annexation.   
 
The proposed annexation is a natural extension of the City, is contiguous to the current City limits, and 
most services typically available to urban areas are available to be extended to this property.  
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the annexation of the property. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Submitted Application 
4. Applicant-submitted annexation map

ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE

ANNEXED TO THE CITY OF CHEYENNE LOCATED EAST OF 
SARATOGA STREET AND SOUTH OF AND ADJACENT TO LARAMIE 
STREET." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That Hilltop Funding, Inc., owners of:  
 
A Parcel Of Land Situate In A Portion Of The Southwest Quarter (Sw¼) Northeast Quarter 
(Ne¼) Southeast Quarter (Se¼) Section 26, T. 14 N.,  R. 66 W., 6th P.M., Laramie County, 
Wyoming, Being More Particularly Described As Follows:  
 
Beginning At The Southeast Corner Of Lot 11, Block 7, Dakota Crossing; Thence N. 
00°17'34"E. Along The Said East Line Of Dakota Crossing A Distance Of 330.33 Feet To 
A Point; Thence S 89°52'00" E. A Distance Of 301.73 Feet To A Point; Thence N. 
00°10'25" E. A Distance Of 299.47 Feet To A Point On The South Line Of Laramie Street; 
Thence N. 89°49'12" W. A Distance Of 302.13 Feet To A Point; Thence N. 00°13'26" E. 
A Distance Of 60.00 Feet To A Point On The North Line Of Laramie Street; Thence  S. 
89°53'31" E. Along The Said North Line A Distance Of 665.03 Feet To A Point; Thence 
S. 89°53'08" E. Continuing Along The Said North Line A Distance Of 624.95 Feet To A 
Point A Point On The West Line Of Whitney Road; Thence S. 00°37'58" E. A Distance Of 
60.00 Feet To A Point On The South Line Of Laramie Street; Thence N. 89°53'20" W. 
Along The Said South Line A Distance Of 624.77 Feet To A Point; Thence S. 00°21'51" 
W. A Distance Of 630.14 Feet To A Point; Thence N. 89°52'52" W. A Distance Of 663.18 
Feet To The Point Of Beginning. 
 
have petitioned the governing body of the City of Cheyenne to annex the real property 
described herein and to make it a part thereof. 
 
 
Section 2.  That Section 2.2.8 of the UDC provides standards and procedures for zoning 
districts established on land or property currently without a City zone assignment including land 
being annexed to the city.  
 
 
Section 3.  That on April 1, 2024, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted 
public hearings, reviewed the proposed zoning assignment that is the subject of this ordinance, and 
recommended approval of said zoning assignment.  
 
 
Section 4.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zoning 
assignment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.8.d of 
the UDC. 
6:13 pm, Apr 01 2024

 
Section 5.  That in accordance with Section 2.2.8, Assigned Zoning, Section 5.1.2, Zoning 
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the zoning 
classifications of the real property as described in Section 1 is hereby established as AR 
Agricultural Residential. 
 
 
Section 6.   That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend 
the Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zoning map 
amendment as described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with 
Section 8 below. 
 
 
Section 7.   Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 8.   That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication, and upon approval and publication of the affiliated annexation.

CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION 
April 1, 2024 
STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
 
PUDC-24-29: Laramie Street – Assigned Zoning 
LOCATION: 
   East of Saratoga St and south of and adjacent to Laramie Street. 
REQUEST: 
  Assigning the AR zone to a property that has petitioned for annexation. 
ANNEXATION 
APPLICANT: 
   Cotton Jones 
OWNER: 
Hilltop Funding Inc. 
ZONING: 
County AR to City AR 
FUTURE LAND USE: 
Mixed-use Residential  
PURPOSE: 
To establish a City zoning district concurrent with annexation. 
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PUDC-24-29 
 
 
April 1, 2024 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve the assigned zoning for land east of Saratoga St. and 
south of and adjacent to Laramie Street to AR Agricultural Residential as shown in the attached zone 
change map, noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.2.8 Assigned Zoning 
 
BACKGROUND: 
The applicant has petitioned for land east of Saratoga St and south of and adjacent to Laramie Street to be 
annexed into city limits. Annexation of a parcel requires a city zone to be established. Per UDC 2.2.8 a zone 
will be assigned based on the underlying county zone and per the policy document adopted by the city. 
Should the applicant wish to have a different zone at the time of annexation, they will be required to submit 
a subsequent zoning map amendment, proposing a different zone. The applicant is not requesting a different 
City zone at this time. 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The assigned zone is the most analogous City zone to the current zone when considering 
allowed uses. Zoning for standard County zones shall be assigned based on the policy 
document, while zones not listed on the policy document and terminated City zones shall be 
assigned a City zone on a case-by-case basis. (UDC 2.2.8.d.1) 
 
The underlying county zone is AR Agricultural Residential. The most analogous city zone is AR 
Agricultural Residential according to the policy document. The Assigned Zoning policy document 
is attached to the staff report as attachment number three.  
 
Establishment of the City zone matches the policy document; therefore, the item complies with 
review criterion 1. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, there were no comments.  
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.8.(d). 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the Assigned Zoning as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the Assigned Zoning subject to conditions or modifications.  

PUDC-24-29 
April 1, 2024 
3.
Recommend denial of the Assigned Zoning based on findings of denial. The Planning
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the
motion, findings for denial must be stated.
4.
Postpone the Assigned Zoning until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved.
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or recommends conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for their recommendation. 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
Staff recommends approval of the assigned zoning for land located east of Saratoga St. and south of and 
adjacent to Laramie Street to AR Agricultural Residential as shown in the attached zone change map, 
noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
ATTACHMENTS:

Zoning Map
2.
Assigned Zoning Policy Document
3.
Use Differences

licensed in the State of Wyoming, PLS Number 9834; 
 
(c) 
That the annexation of said area is for the protection of health, safety, and welfare 
of the persons residing in the area and the City of Cheyenne; 
 
(d) 
That the urban development of the area sought to be annexed will constitute a 
natural, geographical, economical, and social part of the City of Cheyenne; 
 
(e) 
That the area is a logical and feasible addition to the City and that the extension of 
basic services such as water and sewer systems, police and fire protection and 
other services customarily available in the City of Cheyenne can, within reason, 
9:46 am, Apr 03 2024

be furnished; 
 
(f) 
That the area is adjacent to the City of Cheyenne and is currently zoned MR 
Medium Density Residential by the Laramie County Commissioners; and 
 
(g) 
That the City of Cheyenne does not own or operate its own electrical utility, and 
its governing body is prepared to designate a utility to serve the annexed area 
pursuant to W.S. 15-1-410, and to authorize the designated utility to serve the 
entire annexed area. 
 
Section 2.  That all of the real property described in Exhibit A is hereby annexed to the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and shall become a part thereof.  
 
Section 3.  That the City Clerk is directed to file an executed copy of this annexation 
ordinance and map (Exhibit A) with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for 
Laramie County, Wyoming, and shall also notify the City Engineer's Office to add said area to 
the official City Map of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
Section 4.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared 
illegal or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the 
remaining phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.  
 
Section 5.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

STAFF REPORT
FILE:
PUDC-24-28 Toledo Annexation
REQUEST:
Annexation of land 1.60 Acres
LOCATION:
4204 Dell Range Blvd.  
AGENT:
Cotton Jones
OWNERS: 
Gabino Toledo, Na Zhang
PURPOSE: 
Annexation for access to City sewer services
PREPARED BY:
Athen Mores- Planner- Development
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

PUDC-24-28 
 
 
April 8, 2024 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
x 
UDC 2.4.2 Annexations 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting annexation of 1.60 acres of land into the city.  The property is a county pocket 
that is surrounded by 100% city limits.  The land is occupied by 1 home, 1 detached garage, 1 barn, and 5 
other structures ranging from sheds, out buildings, and livestock shelters.  The property is currently zoned as 
MR Medium Density Residential in the County and has a future land use of Urban Residential.   
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
To accept the petition to annex and approve the annexation Ordinance, the Governing Body must make 
the following findings required in WS 15-1-402; a detailed analysis of which is below: 
 
1) An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety and welfare of the 
persons residing in the area and in the city or town.  [WS 15-1-402 (a) (i)] 
 
The annexation of this property will increase the health, safety, and welfare of the currant 
occupants. By being in City limits, the property will receive access to City Fire and Police and 
sanitation services. The property, if occupied, will also be required to be connected to City water 
and sanitary sewer instead of having wells and septic systems.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
2) The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would constitute a natural, 
geographical, economical and social part of the annexing city or town. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (ii)] 
 
This property will constitute a natural, geographic, economic, and social part of the city. The 
property is bordered by 100% city limits in each direction, and is part of a County pocket. The 
contiguity to the City and the proximity to surrounding urban-style development makes the

PUDC-24-28 
 
 
April 8, 2024 
parcel a natural geographic part of the City. The development of this property will benefit those 
who live within the City and will be a part of the social and economic fabric that is Cheyenne.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
3) The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing city or town 
and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents of the city or 
town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed. [WS 15-1-402 (a) 
(iii)] 
 
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City 
services normally available to residents will also be made available. Police and fire protection 
will be extended upon annexation. Utilities exist in the area and will be expanded into the 
proposed area as it develops. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city or town, or 
the area meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (iv)] 
 
The area proposed for annexation is 100% contiguous.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
5) If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body is 
prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (v)] 
 
Utility companies are in place to serve the annexed area.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
    
6) The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all landowners and affected public 
utilities within the territory a summary of the proposed annexation report as required under 
subsection (c) of this section and notice of the time, date and location of the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a). [WS 15-1-402 (a) (vi)] 
 
A certified mailing was mailed to the landowners and public utilities on March 8, 2024. The 
public hearing for this annexation is scheduled for April 8, 2024. Twenty (20) business days (on 
the City’s calendar) prior to the announced public hearing is March 11, 2024. The earliest 
confirmation of receipt was dated March 11, 2024. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
Plan Cheyenne 
 

PUDC-24-28 
 
 
April 8, 2024 
Policies within PlanCheyenne encourage the annexation of County pockets. This is part of a County 
Pocket.    
 
The Future Land Use Map of PlanCheyenne, as updated by Connect 2045, anticipates this area of the 
community to develop with urban density residential development. These areas are expected to 
develop with full access to urban services – such areas are best served by being within City limits. The 
area is within the Urban Service Boundary. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, no comments were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the annexation.   
 
The proposed annexation is a natural extension of the City, is contiguous to the current City limits, and 
most services typically available to urban areas are available to be extended to this property.  
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the annexation of the property. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Submitted Application 
4. Applicant-submitted annexation map

have petitioned the governing body of the City of Cheyenne to annex the real property

Section 2.  That Section 2.2.8 of the UDC provides standards and procedures for zoning 
districts established on land or property currently without a City zone assignment including land 
being annexed to the city.  
 
 
Section 3.  That on April 1, 2024, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted 
public hearings, reviewed the proposed zoning assignment that is the subject of this ordinance, and 
recommended approval of said zoning assignment.  
 
 
Section 4.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zoning 
assignment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.8.d of 
the UDC. 
 
 
Section 5.  That in accordance with Section 2.2.8, Assigned Zoning, Section 5.1.2, Zoning 
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the zoning

classifications of the real property as described in Section 1 is hereby established as MR Medium 
Density Residential. 
 
 
Section 6.   That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend 
the Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zoning map 
amendment as described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with 
Section 8 below. 
 
 
Section 7.   Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 8.   That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication, and upon approval and publication of the affiliated annexation.

STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
 
PUDC-24-28 Toledo Annexation, Assigned Zoning 
LOCATION: 
   4204 Dell Range Blvd. 
REQUEST: 
  Assigned zoning for 1.60 acres of land being annexed from County MR to 
City MR. 
ANNEXATION 
APPLICANT: 
   Cotton Jones 
OWNER: 
Gabino Toledo, Na Zhang 
ZONING: 
County MR to City MR 
FUTURE LAND USE: 
Mixed-use Residential  
PURPOSE: 
To establish a City zoning district concurrent with annexation. 
PREPARED BY: 
Athen Mores- Planner
